# Docket — Launch Compliance Scorecard

> **Status**: Re-audited at the launch gate — 399 of 399 requirements adjudicated against observed evidence.
> **Last Updated**: 2026-08-02
> **Machine-readable source of truth**: [`launch-compliance.json`](./launch-compliance.json)

Every requirement below was mechanically derived from the author's production-launch plan — the plan text was decomposed clause by clause, so each row traces back to something the author actually wrote rather than to an interpretation of what he might have meant. Each requirement carries the author's own words in its `sourceQuote` field in the JSON, alongside the `acceptance` criteria that decide whether it is met.

**Every status here is backed by observed evidence, not by an agent's self-report.** The `evidence` column records what was actually run, read, measured, or looked at — a command and its output, a file and line, a live HTTP response, a screenshot that was opened and inspected. Where a verdict rests on something weaker than the acceptance criteria demand, the evidence says so in its own words. No requirement was marked complete because an implementer said it was.

**Statuses mean exactly this:**

| Status         | Meaning                                                                                          |
| -------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `pass`         | The acceptance criteria were met and the evidence demonstrates it.                               |
| `partial`      | Real work exists and some clauses hold, but at least one acceptance clause is unmet or unproven. |
| `fail`         | The capability exists in some form and demonstrably does not meet the requirement.               |
| `not-built`    | The capability, artifact, or test the requirement names does not exist at all.                   |
| `unverifiable` | **Could not be determined either way in this environment.**                                      |

`unverifiable` means exactly that and **must never be read as `pass`**. It marks requirements where the evidence needed to decide was out of reach — a production instance that could not be signed into, an external account whose credentials this repository does not hold, a surface with no data to render. Each of those 9 rows still needs to be settled before launch; none of them is a quiet approval.

---

## Summary

| Metric                          |   Count |
| ------------------------------- | ------: |
| Total requirements              |     399 |
| Pass                            |      94 |
| Partial                         |     122 |
| Fail                            |      85 |
| Not built                       |      89 |
| Unverifiable                    |       9 |
| **Launch blockers outstanding** | **108** |

Severity split across all 399 requirements: **138 launch-blocker**, 221 high, 40 medium. Of the 138 launch-blockers, 108 are outstanding (anything not `pass`) and 30 pass.

---

Mobile defects: the 'Close cycle' button is stretched full-bleed across the viewport, a stray horizontal rule appears at y≈420 separating nothing, the Pace section is silently absent entirely, and the Athena FAB overlaps the empty-state box.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

Of the 3 remaining failures: @docket/web failed 2 of 1681 tests only inside the full 16-task turbo run — re-run standalone (`vitest run --coverage`) it is 206/206 files and 1681/1681 tests green at 95.25/90.89/96.59/98.18, so the turbo-run failure is resource contention between concurrently-scheduled packages, not a real regression. @docket/test-utils failed one real, committed, reproducible test: tests/security/secret-scan.test.ts flags packages/auth/tests/builder/auth.test.ts:1694 and :1722, where the @docket/auth coverage-closing commit (4918a5a6) left a `re_test_forwarded_key` fixture that matches the repo's own resend-api-key secret pattern with no `gitleaks:allow` annotation — a genuine defect, not flakiness or coverage math. @docket/api crashed with `Something removed the coverage directory ... Vitest created earlier` / `ENOENT ... coverage-116.json` — direct evidence of a second, concurrent test:coverage run colliding with this one: `git status` shows apps/api/tests with 4 modified and 17 untracked files and no new commit since f9a5d000, i.e. another agent is actively working on @docket/api's coverage in this shared worktree right now, and that work is entirely uncommitted. Per this task's own instruction not to run @docket/api's coverage in a way that writes files, it was not re-run standalone to get a clean number. @docket/admin and @docket/runner still have no test:coverage script (16 coverage tasks vs 18 test tasks), unchanged from the prior gate. |

## Findings by area

One section per area, in the order the areas appear in the plan. Each table is `id | requirement | severity | status | evidence`; the author's source quote and the full acceptance criteria for every row live in [`launch-compliance.json`](./launch-compliance.json).

### Launch Scope

1 requirement — 1 fail · 1 launch-blocker outstanding.

| ID       | Requirement                                                                                                                                                          | Severity       | Status | Evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| -------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `GEN-01` | Every requirement defined anywhere in the plan document is completed before launch is declared — no item may be deferred, partially shipped, or left as a follow-up. | launch-blocker | `fail` | Final gate 2026-08-02 (branch claude/docket-production-launch-ebe2d9): the launch checklist now exists and is generated — `pnpm launch:record` writes docs/engineering/launch/launch-checklist.md and launch-record.json from the 399-row baseline plus docs/engineering/launch/slices/\*.md. But the acceptance's count is not zero: the generated summary reads `not-started 366 / in-progress 21 / closed 12`, `Launch-blockers not closed 133`, `Sign-off gate violations 387`, and `Sign-off: withheld`. Only four slice files were ever filed (ci-gating, launch-governance, security-and-domains, test-standards) even though ~1048 files changed across 45 commits, so most shipped work claims no requirement id at all. |

The reachable Athena components confirm this: `grep -i "mcp\|connect\|tool" athena-workspace.tsx athena-workbench.tsx` finds only one unrelated doc-comment. |

Entity mutations all live behind tool modules instead — `grep -rn "insert(task)" apps/api/src` shows apps/api/src/mcp/write-tools.ts:85, mcp/content-tools.ts:280, mcp/organize-tool.ts:628 plus non-agent routes. MISSING: no architecture/import-graph test exists — `grep -rln "import graph\|importGraph\|architecture test\|no write repositor"` over apps/api/tests and packages returns zero hits, and apps/api/tests/agent/ contains no such spec. |

Schema permits it (agentSession.taskId nullable, agents.ts:83; agentSessionRun has no task column at all). Demonstrated live: I created session 01KZ0T28RF4STDS0P46798ZSRD via `POST $API_URL/v1/me/athena/chat/new` with no Docket work item, and `GET $API_URL/v1/me/athena` shows it plus four pre-existing `kind:job` sessions (01KZ0T25C3MTM2TM6EH3X34VBE, 01KZ0SHKT0J661TXDBT9QQKD4E, 01KZ0SEK283W1079J677ZM2Z2F, 01KZ0SEK15YQ6PT9P4MZE1ZB1X) in awaiting_approval. No test asserts zero null task references: `grep -rln "taskId.*not.*null\|null task reference" apps/api/tests` → zero hits. The MCP capture path inserts a task with no parent: apps/api/src/mcp/write-tools.ts:85-100 builds `.insert(task).values({organizationId, title, description, teamId: landing.teamId, state: landing.state, assigneeId, cycleId, source:'native', createdBy})` — projectId/programId/milestoneId are never set and no candidate parent is searched for. The system prompt gives no linking guidance: `grep -n -i "project\|initiative\|objective\|parent\|link" apps/api/src/agent/system-prompt.ts` yields only :84 ('projects, and views with your read tools first, then act') and :91 ('Create parent containers (projects) in an earlier turn than …') — both about creating NEW parents, not attaching to existing ones. No fixture test exists: `grep -rln "plausible parent\|could not find a parent\|linked to the correct parent"` over apps, packages and docs → zero hits.                                                                               |

| `CORE-35` | A single universal timer can be started from anywhere in the app and tracks the task currently being worked on.                      | launch-blocker | `not-built` | There is no time-tracking UI anywhere in the web app. `grep -rniE 'v1/time\|timeRecord\|TimeActive\|useTimer\|startTimer' apps/web/src --include=*.ts --include=*.tsx` (excluding datetime/timezone/wall-time false positives) returns ZERO matches; same grep against apps/admin/src returns zero. No /time route exists in the route listing (find apps/web/src/app -name page.tsx). Confirmed visually — no timer control on Today (apps/web/.data/design-review/audit-baseline/today-1440x900-light.png), Athena (athena-1440x900-light.png), or task detail (audit-taskdetail/…-1440x900-light.png).                                                                     |
| `CORE-36` | The running timer can be paused and resumed.                                                                                         | launch-blocker | `partial`   | Backend verified working via REST against the live API: POST /v1/time/records → id 01KZ0S1ME4H0PWAYQAFG1AHK3D, then pause/start/pause/start each returned HTTP 200, and the final stop response shows intervals correctly bounded (first interval endedAt 08:22:01.480Z, second startedAt 08:22:02.522Z endedAt 08:22:03.560Z) — accrual halts on pause and resumes on start. Implementation at apps/api/src/time/commands.ts:325-350 (pauseTimeRecord closes the active interval) and :250-322 (startTimeRecord opens a new one). No UI: see CORE-35 evidence.                                                                                                               |
| `CORE-37` | Each contiguous stretch of tracked time is persisted as its own individual segment record.                                           | launch-blocker | `pass`      | Drove the exact acceptance sequence against the live API (start→pause→resume→pause→resume→stop) on record 01KZ0S1ME4H0PWAYQAFG1AHK3D. The stop response returned exactly 3 non-superseded intervals, each with its own bounded start/end: 01KZ0S1ME7FT9ZBGG6PMCXK2BN (08:22:01.411→08:22:01.480), 01KZ0S1NGWBQMWS5FNWCTZDFT8 (08:22:02.522→08:22:03.560), 01KZ0S1QHMYZKJCXK988JY9TER. Retrievable via GET /v1/time/records — no interval spans a paused gap. Schema backing: packages/db/src/schema/time.ts:101-133 `timeInterval` with its own startedAt/endedAt.                                                                                                            |
| `CORE-38` | Every recorded segment is associated with the specific activity/task it was tracking.                                                | launch-blocker | `fail`      | `timeInterval` has NO task column at all — packages/db/src/schema/time.ts:101-133 references timeRecordId (notNull), hubId, userId, agentExecutionId; there is no taskId and therefore no NOT NULL foreign key to a task. Task association is an OPTIONAL, separate `timeContext` row (time.ts:136-162), and it is explicitly non-counting. Proven live: my probe record 01KZ0S1ME4H0PWAYQAFG1AHK3D tracked 3 segments and returned `"contexts":[],"allocations":[]` — a timer ran to completion with zero task association.                                                                                                                                                  |
| `CORE-39` | Restarting the timer on the same task within under a minute automatically joins the segments instead of recording a break.           | high           | `not-built` | No merge logic exists. apps/api/src/time/commands.ts:250-322 `startTimeRecord` unconditionally inserts a fresh `timeInterval` row (:304-312) with no gap inspection — there is no read of the previous interval's endedAt and no 60-second constant anywhere in apps/api/src/time/. Proven live: my pause→resume cycles had ~1-second gaps (interval 1 ended 08:22:01.480, interval 2 started 08:22:02.522) and produced 2 SEPARATE segments rather than 1 merged span.                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `CORE-40` | A timer can be started for any task, at any time, directly from that task.                                                           | high           | `not-built` | No start-timer affordance exists in any task representation. Task detail (apps/web/.data/design-review/audit-taskdetail/orgs-orgId-tasks-01KZ0S5VFWFNHTNEW95DTD2K94-1440x900-light.png) shows only Backlog / No priority / Assign / Set due date / Have Athena handle this — no timer control. The tasks table (audit-baseline/tasks-1440x900-light.png) and calendar (audit-baseline/calendar-1440x900-light.png) likewise show none. Root cause is CORE-35: zero web references to the time API.                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `CORE-41` | The UI must block finishing a tracking session until the task being tracked has a name.                                              | launch-blocker | `not-built` | There is no timer UI to enforce a naming prompt in — no time-tracking component exists in apps/web/src (zero matches for the time API across all .ts/.tsx; no /time route among the 70 page.tsx files found under apps/web/src/app). With no stop control, there is no naming dialog, no disabled-confirm-while-empty state, and no Escape behaviour to test.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `CORE-42` | The system — not just the UI — refuses to close a tracking session whose task has no name.                                           | launch-blocker | `partial`   | Tested the exact bypass the requirement names, straight at the REST API with no UI involved. Whitespace title at creation: POST /v1/time/records with `{"context":{"label":"   "}}` → HTTP 422 `{"code":"validation_error","fieldErrors":{"context.label":[{"code":"too_small","minimum":1}]}}`. Renaming to blank: PATCH /v1/time/records/{id} with `{"title":"   "}` → 422, and with `{"title":""}` → 422 (same code). Backed by packages/types/src/time.ts:93 `label: z.string().trim().min(1).max(500)`. Stop then succeeded on the still-validly-named record.                                                                                                           |
| `CORE-43` | The task created or named during time tracking is a first-class Docket task, not a tracking-only record.                             | high           | `fail`      | A separate timer-only entity type is exactly what exists: packages/db/src/schema/time.ts:68-98 defines `timeRecord` (hubId, createdByUserId, title, outcomeNote, status, categoryId, captureSource…) as its own table, distinct from the work-item task table in packages/db/src/schema/work.ts. My probe record 01KZ0S1ME4H0PWAYQAFG1AHK3D was returned by GET /v1/time/records with a TimeRecordOut shape (intervals/contexts/allocations/measures) and does NOT appear in GET /v1/orgs/{orgId}/tasks, which returned only the 5 'Audit Task NN' rows with the TaskOut shape (state, priority, assigneeId, projectId, teamId…).                                             |
| `CORE-44` | Timer lifecycle transitions emit observable events that Athena can consume.                                                          | launch-blocker | `not-built` | The time module emits nothing. `grep -rn 'emit\|publish\|event' apps/api/src/time/*.ts` returns only two unrelated `calendar_event` string comparisons (access.ts:152, :262). apps/api/src/time/commands.ts imports no emitter — its imports (lines 10-39) are drizzle, @docket/db, @docket/types, ../error, ./access, ./read-models. By contrast `emitEvent` IS used by tasks, projects, comments, initiatives, automation and email-to-task (apps/api/src/routes/tasks.ts, projects.ts, comments.ts, initiatives.ts, lib/task-state.ts, lib/automation/\*) — so the event substrate exists and time is simply not wired into it.                                            |
| `CORE-45` | An external website can display the user's currently-tracked task via a supported public read interface.                             | high           | `not-built` | No public read interface. Unauthenticated GET /v1/time/active → HTTP 401 `{"code":"unauthorized","title":"Sign in required."}`. Cross-origin check with `Origin: https://example.com` returned 401 and NO `access-control-allow-origin` header (only access-control-allow-credentials and access-control-expose-headers). The route is session-only by design — apps/api/src/routes/time.ts:76 documents it as 'Session-only; raw time remains personal to the caller's Hub', and apps/api/src/app.ts:55 gates every /v1 route on a session. No widget script, user-scoped token, or embed snippet exists.                                                                    |
| `CORE-46` | The time tracker is fully controllable through MCP.                                                                                  | high           | `not-built` | The MCP tool inventory contains no timer tools. Registration is enumerated at apps/api/src/mcp/tools.ts:53-64 (content, session, view-plan, write, update, organize, link, archive, plan, athena-assignment, workspaces) and the concrete names are: archive, brief, capture, comment, find, get, link, link*external, list_work, manage_session, organize, plan_day, report_status, run_agent, undo, update, workspaces. `grep -rniE "name:\s\*'[a-z*]_(time\|timer\|track)[a-z\_]_'" apps/api/src/mcp/\*.ts` returns nothing. Corroborated independently by the live Docket MCP server exposed to this session, whose tool list matches exactly and includes no timer tool. |
| `CORE-47` | The time tracker is fully controllable through the Docket REST API.                                                                  | high           | `pass`      | Fetched the live spec: GET /v1/openapi.json lists 17 time paths including /v1/time/records [post], /v1/time/records/{id}/start [post], /pause [post], /stop [post], /intervals [post], /contexts [post], /v1/time/active [get], /v1/time/timeline [get], /v1/time/summary [get], /v1/time/breakdown [get] — each with request/response schemas and descriptions (apps/api/src/routes/time.ts:69-344 apiDoc blocks). Exercised successfully end-to-end: create → HTTP 200 with id, start/pause ×4 → HTTP 200 each, stop → HTTP 200 returning 3 intervals, timeline/breakdown → HTTP 200 with reconciling totals.                                                               |
| `CORE-48` | A dedicated time-tracking analytics surface exists with a clear, simple UI in the spirit of Sunsama and Toggl Track.                 | high           | `not-built` | No analytics surface exists. The route inventory (find apps/web/src/app -name page.tsx, 70 routes) contains no time or analytics page, and zero web files reference the time API (see CORE-35). The backing data IS available — GET /v1/time/breakdown and /v1/time/summary both return populated measures — but nothing renders them: no period selector, no period total, no ranked breakdown, no empty state.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `CORE-49` | Tracked time can be broken down by project, program, initiative, and workspace, with totals that reconcile to the recorded segments. | high           | `partial`   | Hit GET /v1/time/breakdown live for all seven dimensions. Working: workspace, project, task, category, actor — each returned buckets with reconciling totals (e.g. workspace → `{"key":"unallocated:workspace","label":"Unallocated","measures":{"elapsedMs":4319,"humanEffortMs":2241,…}}`). Failing: `groupBy=program` → HTTP 422 `fieldErrors.groupBy [{code:'invalid_option'}]`, and `groupBy=initiative` → HTTP 422 same. Root cause: packages/types/src/time.ts:318 `TimeBreakdownDimension = z.enum(['workspace','task','project','category','actor'])`.                                                                                                               |

### PWA

| `CAL-31` | Docket must be a fully installable PWA.                                                                        | high     | `partial` | Verified live in production by curl: `https://docket.hypertext.studio/manifest.webmanifest` returns 200 with name 'Docket', short_name 'Docket', start_url '/today', scope '/', display 'standalone', and four icons — 192/512 `any` plus 192/512 `maskable`. `/sw.js` → 200 application/javascript. `/icons/icon-192.png` → 200. `/apple-icon.png?...` → 200, and the served HTML contains `<link rel="manifest" href="/manifest.webmanifest">` and `<link rel="apple-touch-icon" ... sizes="180x180">`. The worker has a fetch handler (apps/web/service-worker/sw.ts:97).                                                                                                   |
| `CAL-32` | PWA features must be layered on via progressive enhancement so the app is fully functional without them.       | high     | `partial` | Feature detection is present: apps/web/src/components/service-worker-provider.tsx:63 guards with `if (!('serviceWorker' in navigator)) return undefined;` and apps/web/src/lib/query-persist.ts:64-65 `canPersistQueries()` returns `typeof window !== 'undefined' && typeof window.indexedDB !== 'undefined'`. Registration failures are deliberately swallowed (documented at service-worker-provider.tsx:35-38), so no service-worker text reaches the UI.                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `CAL-33` | Previously visited surfaces must render from cache when the device is offline.                                 | high     | `pass`    | Final gate 2026-08-02 (branch claude/docket-production-launch-ebe2d9): apps/web/tests/service-worker/documents.test.ts passes and asserts previously visited surfaces render from cache offline: navigateWithDocumentCache 'brings a previously-visited route back offline instead of the waiting room', does not touch the network when the browser knows it is offline and the route is cached, falls back to the waiting room only for a route never visited, and never serves one account's shell to another (documents are keyed on the signed-in account and purged on account change and sign-out). apps/web/e2e/platform/pwa-offline.spec.ts covers it in the browser. |
| `CAL-34` | Mutations made while offline must be queued locally and replayed automatically on reconnect.                   | high     | `pass`    | Final gate 2026-08-02 (branch claude/docket-production-launch-ebe2d9): apps/web/tests/pwa/offline-queue.test.ts and outbox-model.test.ts pass and cover the acceptance: an undeliverable API write is queued and survives a reload, reads and auth traffic are never queued, a server error is never masked as an offline one, and drainOutbox replays every queued write in the order it was made with no user action, sending the original method, body and content type. apps/web/e2e/platform/pwa-offline-sync.spec.ts is the browser-level spec.                                                                                                                          |
| `CAL-35` | When data syncing is involved, the UI must clearly say the functionality resumes once the user is back online. | high     | `pass`    | Final gate 2026-08-02 (branch claude/docket-production-launch-ebe2d9): apps/web/tests/pwa/offline-sync-indicator.test.tsx passes and asserts the UI says it: the indicator states that changes are held here and will sync on reconnect, gives every pending change its own marker, clears them once they reach the server, offers a way out for a change the server refused, promises a sync only while one is actually coming, and never leaks provider or exception text.                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `CAL-36` | Dedicated Liquid Glass icons must be shipped for Apple platforms.                                              | medium   | `pass`    | Final gate 2026-08-02 (branch claude/docket-production-launch-ebe2d9): apps/web/tests/pwa/apple-icons.test.ts passes and asserts the Apple asset set ships every size iOS asks for at the size it claims and is distinct artwork rather than the standard icon renamed, with the source document declaring the Liquid Glass treatment rather than a flat image.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `CAL-37` | Adding the Apple icons must not alter or degrade icons on other platforms.                                     | medium   | `pass`    | Final gate 2026-08-02 (branch claude/docket-production-launch-ebe2d9): the same suite asserts non-interference directly: the manifest stays free of Apple assets and the two generators have disjoint outputs, so neither can touch the other's icons.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `CAL-38` | The Apple icons must be produced with Apple's Icon Composer app, with the source document kept in the repo.    | medium   | `pass`    | Final gate 2026-08-02 (branch claude/docket-production-launch-ebe2d9): the same suite asserts the source document is an Icon Composer document, is committed, is what the exports come from, and is rendered by Apple's exporter rather than by this repository.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `CAL-39` | The Apple icon artwork must use the full canvas per Apple's safe padding/margin guidance.                      | medium   | `pass`    | Final gate 2026-08-02 (branch claude/docket-production-launch-ebe2d9): the same suite measures the grid: the artwork fills the canvas (the mask reaches all four edges), uses most of the live area rather than floating in the middle, is centred on both axes, and never touches the mask edge.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

The gateway is the local stub, so the api-call acceptance against the real Lattice gateway is unmet.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
